# Research: subset-b-000605

Grouped research for ceph-client Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering pinctrl, pmem, power-domain, reset, and power-supply bindings. Each section preserves the source path and is wrapped for deterministic reconciliation into source-tree-aligned per-file reports.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pinctrl/st,stm32-pinctrl.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `STM32 GPIO and Pin Mux/Config controller`. In this tree it is a pin controller / pinmux / GPIO binding schema; it constrains the DTS/DTB ABI used to describe hardware to Linux and to the dt-schema validation tools. The file was read as a complete 383-line YAML schema, including its property definitions, composition rules, examples, and closure policy. The source description says: STMicroelectronics's STM32 MCUs integrate a GPIO and Pin mux/config hardware controller.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This file is declarative schema, not executable kernel code, so its public surface is the binding ABI: `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/pinctrl/st,stm32-pinctrl.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `maintainers`, `description`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and `examples`. Compatible strings gathered from the full schema are `st,stm32f429-pinctrl`, `st,stm32f469-pinctrl`, `st,stm32f746-pinctrl`, `st,stm32f769-pinctrl`, `st,stm32h743-pinctrl`, `st,stm32mp135-pinctrl`, `st,stm32mp157-pinctrl`, `st,stm32mp157-z-pinctrl`, `st,stm32mp257-pinctrl`, `st,stm32mp257-z-pinctrl`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`; nested or conditional required properties are `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`, `reg`, `clocks`, `st,bank-name`, `gpio-hog`, `pinmux`, `skew-delay-input-ps`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`, `pins-are-numbered`, `hwlocks`, `interrupts`, `st,syscfg`, `st,package`. Property roles include `compatible` (driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier); `#address-cells` (child-node address encoding); `#size-cells` (child-node size encoding); `interrupts` (interrupt routing and wake/error signaling); `gpio-controller` (marks the node as a GPIO provider); `#gpio-cells` (GPIO provider specifier width); `#interrupt-cells` (provider cell-count contract); `reg` (MMIO, bus address, or reserved-memory range descriptor); `clocks` (clock provider phandles consumed by the driver); `resets` (reset-controller phandles for hardware reset lines); `gpio-ranges` (maps GPIO line numbers to pinctrl pins). Direct and nested `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/pinctrl/pincfg-node.yaml`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32-array`, `pinctrl.yaml#`. Local `$defs`/`definitions` are none. Top-level closure is `additionalProperties: False`. Maintainers: Alexandre TORGUE <alexandre.torgue@foss.st.com>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling, the OF core, and the matched Linux subsystem driver rather than by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the declared meta-schema, resolves references such as `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/pinctrl/pincfg-node.yaml`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32-array`, `pinctrl.yaml#`, evaluates properties, `required` lists, enum/const/item constraints, `patternProperties`, local definitions, and composition keywords, then validates the inline examples. At boot, a compiled DT node matching one of the compatible strings allows OF/platform/I2C/syscon/firmware code to bind the relevant driver; the driver and subsystem framework then read pin groups/functions, GPIO ranges, mux values, bias/drive settings, interrupt parents, and SoC-specific register banks. Schema composition points present in this file are `allOf`, `if`, `then`, `not`. Patterned child-node surfaces are `^gpio@[0-9a-f]*$`, `-[0-9]*$`, `^(.+-hog(-[0-9]+)?)$`, `^pins`.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence impact is ABI stability: board DTS files and shipped DTBs permanently encode compatible strings, register ordering, provider cell counts, child-node names, phandle topology, and SoC- or device-specific property values validated here. Tightening `required`, changing enums, altering `#*-cells`, or flipping `additionalProperties: False` can break existing boards even though the file itself is static documentation plus validation rules.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrated with Linux pinctrl, pinmux, pinconf, and GPIO integration. It depends on dt-schema core handling plus the referenced schemas listed above; examples also rely on standard DTS include files and binding headers where present. Driver integration is through `compatible`, while framework integration is through standard OF properties, provider/client cell contracts, child-node topology, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, GPIOs, IIO/NVMEM/syscon phandles, and any SoC-specific properties declared in this schema.

**Risks:** Main risks are binding ABI drift, missing or overly broad compatible coverage, incorrect top-level or conditional required resources, wrong provider cell counts, examples that miss conditional branches, and closure settings that either reject valid legacy DTS extensions or allow misspelled properties. For this file, pay particular attention to pin groups/functions, GPIO ranges, mux values, bias/drive settings, interrupt parents, and SoC-specific register banks; these are the fields most likely to couple board data to driver assumptions and SoC register layout.

**Test signals:** Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pinctrl/st,stm32-pinctrl.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` against DTS users of every compatible family listed above. Useful failure signals include unresolved `$ref`s, example compilation errors, missing required-property diagnostics, unexpected additional-property acceptance/rejection, enum/const mismatches for compatible-specific branches, and probe-time failures in the matched Linux driver or framework. This file contains 2 top-level `examples` block(s), which are part of the dt-schema validation signal.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pinctrl/st,stm32-pinctrl.yaml -->

At boot, a compiled DT node matching one of the compatible strings allows OF/platform/I2C/syscon/firmware code to bind the relevant driver; the driver and subsystem framework then read power-domain cells, clocks, resets, register ranges, idle states, performance states, and provider/client phandle contracts. Schema composition points present in this file are none. Patterned child-node surfaces are none.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence impact is ABI stability: board DTS files and shipped DTBs permanently encode compatible strings, register ordering, provider cell counts, child-node names, phandle topology, and SoC- or device-specific property values validated here. Tightening `required`, changing enums, altering `#*-cells`, or flipping `additionalProperties: True` can break existing boards even though the file itself is static documentation plus validation rules.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrated with Linux generic PM domain, runtime PM, genpd providers, firmware power domains, and SoC reset/clock integration. It depends on dt-schema core handling plus the referenced schemas listed above; examples also rely on standard DTS include files and binding headers where present. Driver integration is through `compatible`, while framework integration is through standard OF properties, provider/client cell contracts, child-node topology, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, GPIOs, IIO/NVMEM/syscon phandles, and any SoC-specific properties declared in this schema.

**Risks:** Main risks are binding ABI drift, missing or overly broad compatible coverage, incorrect top-level or conditional required resources, wrong provider cell counts, examples that miss conditional branches, and closure settings that either reject valid legacy DTS extensions or allow misspelled properties. For this file, pay particular attention to power-domain cells, clocks, resets, register ranges, idle states, performance states, and provider/client phandle contracts; these are the fields most likely to couple board data to driver assumptions and SoC register layout.

**Test signals:** Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/power-domain.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` against DTS users of every compatible family listed above. Useful failure signals include unresolved `$ref`s, example compilation errors, missing required-property diagnostics, unexpected additional-property acceptance/rejection, enum/const mismatches for compatible-specific branches, and probe-time failures in the matched Linux driver or framework. This file contains 3 top-level `examples` block(s), which are part of the dt-schema validation signal.
